R156-12 names only `/runs/**` plus the chart, so the endpoint boundary is incomplete. [PROJECT EVIDENCE] |
| Unqualified `@Async` and no Replay executor | **Confirmed, with a concrete conflict.** `RegressionTestWorker.java:99` uses plain `@Async`, and there is no `AsyncConfigurer`. Production's `DynamicCoinActivationService.java:18, 35` also uses plain `@Async`, so Replay runs share a default pool with Production coin-activation work. This is the exact thing R156-02 forbids ("refuse capacity rather than use Production capacity"). [PROJECT EVIDENCE + AGENT INFERENCE about Spring Boot's default executor] |
| "Replay only" conflicts with editing shared classes | **Confirmed and stronger than stated.** `ExecutionReplayScope` is referenced by 13 non-Replay classes, including `TradeExecutionValidationService`, `BtcMarketContextService`, `MultiTimeframeConfluenceService`, `OpportunityConsolidationService`, `EntryConsumptionPolicy`, `ExecutionPriceAuthorityService`, `StopLossEvidenceService` and `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore`. Several branch on `replayScope.active()`. R156-05 (scope propagation, fail-closed) and R156-06 (price-event projection) therefore require edits to classes Production runs. A1's claim that Production source does not change can't hold as written. The specification must allow those edits explicitly and require zero-behaviour-change proof (the Production path is byte-equivalent when scope is inactive). Otherwise it must use new Replay-only types. [PROJECT EVIDENCE + AGENT INFERENCE] |
| The fixture scope for analysis parity is incomplete | **Confirmed.** `AnalysisService.buildSignal` reads more than D53 (sentiment) and D54 (prior indicator snapshot) cover: the current `sentimentService.isEnabled()` flag (`:227`), fundamentals with an availability rule (`fundamentalService.latestAsOf`/`isAvailable`, `:234-235`), ATR-risk configuration (`:266`) and the regime services (`:270-272`). The register has no row for fundamentals availability, the sentiment enable flag, or the scoring/ATR/regime configuration. [PROJECT EVIDENCE] |

## OBJECTIONS

1. **Production-source impact.** The frozen specification must list every Production-shared class it edits and require a no-behaviour-change regression test for each one with scope inactive. Otherwise A1 must be withdrawn.
2. **Endpoint boundary.** R156-12 and T156-11 must list every route under `/api/administration/regression-tests`: in boundary, read-only legacy, or retired.
3. **Run identity.** Register an identity that survives a purge, such as a UUID or content hash, for certificates, logs and proven records. Alternatively, stop the AUTO_INCREMENT reset for `analysis_test_run`.
4. **Executor.** R156-02 must name a dedicated executor bean with queue size and rejection behaviour, and say how scope is carried to async children (T156-14).
5. **Analysis fixtures.** Add register rows for fundamentals availability, the sentiment enable flag, and the ATR/regime/scoring configuration, each with an as-of fixture or a refusal rule.
6. **FIX-154.** Decide whether FIX-154 BUY evaluation is mirrored or excluded.
7. **Freeze sequencing.** RPR-0.9.12 must exist at the canonical path before P01. The F-3/F-4 sources, the F-8 feasibility check and the F-9 drift guard must be resolved before freeze.

- RPR-0.9.11/0.9.12 contents and hashes. I couldn't compute hashes, and I didn't re-read the Downloads copies this round.

## FINDINGS FOR JEAN

1. **Not ready to freeze or implement.** Codex and I agree. All gates P01Г?"P07 are Pending, E03Г?"E10 are missing, and the canonical specification is still RPR-0.4.
2. **I corrected two of my own Round-1 points.** Price ordering is already correct (the ID is simply dropped, not mis-sorted). And D48 option (a) is your recorded owner decision, not an unauthorised change. It still has to go into RPR-0.9.12 before freeze.
3. **The "no Production source changes" claim won't hold.** The Replay scope is threaded through 13 Production classes, so the fixes in R156-05 and R156-06 touch Production code. Choose one:
   - allow those edits explicitly, with tests proving nothing changes when Replay isn't running; or
   - build new Replay-only types instead.
4. **The boundary misses many routes.** The `/proven-trades` and `/archives` routes, plus five per-fix diagnostics controllers, sit outside the planned `/runs/**` boundary.
5. **Replay shares a thread pool with Production.** Replay currently runs on the default async pool alongside Production coin activation. It needs its own named executor.
6. **Deleting test data recycles run IDs.** After a delete, new run IDs can collide with kept proven trades and with old certificates. You need a run identity that survives a purge.
7. **Decide the historical source for settings and exchange minimums, or refuse those runs.** Today there is no history for either. The same applies to the extra analysis inputs (fundamentals, sentiment on/off, ATR/regime configuration) and to the FIX-154 BUY path.

VERDICT: REJECTED

# FIX-156 - Replay/Production Parity: Consolidated Preparation and Final Pre-Implementation Review

**Revision:** Consolidated preparation, E01/E02 draft evidence, and final static code review, 2026-10-15  
**Status:** NOT APPROVED FOR IMPLEMENTATION  
**Prepared:** 2026-10-11 KSA  
**Source design:** owner-provided `FIX-156-RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT.md`  
**RPR-0.9.12 SHA-256:** `ddfd5f64a95f6b55ed39e8bffa7b4c93bd65f4e6dd3b23d8bf821500de722b24`  
**Owner decision O-8:** RPR-0.9.12 incorporates option (a), the narrowly scoped `fix122_evaluation` exception in its Section 2.1 and in D48/R156-05/T156-09b.  
**This document review SHA-256:** Pending final freeze; calculate and record it in the review request only after the last content edit.  
**Application/configuration/migration/database/service/deployment changes made by this document:** NONE

## 1. Fixed scope and earned results

FIX-156 changes **Replay only**. It does not change Production source,
behaviour, database writes, account state, schedulers, HTTP traffic, or
latency. Replay is a manual single-symbol research tool with fixed notional
output. It is not a wallet, balance, cash, reserve, budget, portfolio, or
historical-fill simulator.

A run has two independent earned certification dimensions:

- `ANALYSIS_PARITY`: a qualified Production signal/outcome is reproduced
  using as-of evidence.
- `LIFECYCLE_PARITY`: a qualified recorded-entry lifecycle passes the
  separate lifecycle metric and critical-safety gate.

A run may earn neither, one, or both dimensions. `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`
and `FAILED_REPLAY` are terminal states which prevent either claim.
`FAILED_REPLAY` is only for a technical error, cancellation, or failed
staging. A business mismatch is published as parity-not-earned with its first
divergence; it is not a technical failure.

No result may claim wallet, account, exact-fill, live-scheduling, MySQL-lock,
or Binance-availability parity.

## 2. Decided owner rules

| Decision | Implementable rule |
| --- | --- |
| O-1 | Replay never calls `wallet_settings` or shared `validateBuy`. A Replay-local BUY mirror reads only a frozen policy profile. |
| O-2 | Signal and lifecycle parity are separate certification dimensions. |
| O-3 | Every class reachable from a Replay request or worker is inside the boundary. |
| O-4 | The 99% result is separate per metric, based on a frozen lower confidence bound, coverage floor, and critical-safety gate. |
| O-5 | A lifecycle fixture carries the full recorded timeline, not entry only. |
| O-6 | No separate Replay database identity/datasource is introduced. |
| O-7 | No shared-datasource SQL guard is introduced. Replay safety is application/test enforced, not a database-permission claim. |
| O-8 / D48 | Replay may use `fix122_evaluation` only through the exact run-owned and display-only operations in Section 4.2a. This exception never supplies decision, wallet, account, fill, or Production-trading authority. |

New Replay settings are disabled by default and define numeric limits for workers, queue, fixture size, price span, duration, artifact size and retention. Every new table records owner, retention, indexes and rollback note. Verify schema before a run. | `RUN_REFUSED` or `RUN_FAILED` with schema/resource reason |
| R156-14 | Unit/integration/fixture/architecture tests | Implement the numbered test set in Section 7; no silently skipped mandatory evidence. | Test evidence uses the registry events where runtime applies |
| R156-15 | Documentation/registry | Reconcile approved registry to actual implementation, then update required architecture/changelog/rules/investigation/validation documents. | Implementation report, not an application log |

### 4.2a `fix122_evaluation` transitional shared-table exception and delete policy

`fix122_evaluation` is a shared diagnostics/evidence table which Production
also writes. The approved D48 boundary exception is deliberately narrow: it
supports run-owned Replay diagnostic evidence plus one read-only Production
diagnostic display. It cannot participate in a Replay decision, parity
classification, qualification, entry, exit, sizing, or lifecycle transition.

The permitted run-owned operations are:

```sql
DELETE FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NOT NULL
```

For the eventual baseline, this is an explicit table allow-list exception, not
an untracked boundary violation. The allow-list and static reachability tests
must permit only the following exact operations:

- read, insert, and query rows whose `test_run_id` identifies the active
  Replay run;
- the scoped purge above, never an unqualified delete and never a delete of a
  row where `test_run_id IS NULL`;
- the existing read-only display query `SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE
  test_run_id IS NULL AND symbol=? AND evaluated_at>=? AND evaluated_at<=?
  ORDER BY id`. It may populate diagnostics display only and must not feed a
  decision, parity result, fixture, or any mutable state;
- no other select, insert, update, delete, repository, JDBC template, or
  indirect access to this table.

The direct and background-task tests must fail the build for any operation
outside this exact policy. Any other shared-table access remains forbidden.
D48 remains open until the implemented policy and its direct/background-task
tests prove these limits. RPR-0.9.12 adopts this owner decision. It remains a
draft and is not an implementation authorization.

| RET-02 | Wallet-shaped `*_test` data as a baseline dependency or output. | Preserve historic data/read-only viewers; write Replay-owned output only. |
| RET-03 | Direct `wallet_trade` read in the Replay chart. | Replay output plus immutable fixture overlay. |
| RET-04 | Zero-price-event signal-price fallback labelled exact. | Refuse missing price coverage. |
| RET-05 | FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, pre-FIX-122 and `EXPERIMENTAL` baseline invocation. | Display-only legacy history; separate future experiment entry point if authorised. |
| RET-06 | 13-field `ReplayParityValidator` as a certificate. | Versioned comparator for D01-D46, D47-D56 and S01-S14. |
| RET-07 | Unclassified lifecycle beyond `executionEnd`. | Never create a synthetic exit. Refuse incomplete final-tail coverage. `OPEN_AT_REPLAY_END` stores the final qualified mark price and its event ID/time and is censored only when Production is also open. Replay-open/Production-closed is a published mismatch. |
| RET-08 | Shared BUY validation and live Binance minimum-order call. | Replay-local mirror plus frozen policy/exchange-constraint fixtures. |

## 6. Mandatory logging registry

There is one registry only. Every Replay log starts
`[FIX-156][REPLAY]`, includes `runId`, `profileHash`, `baselineHash`,
`symbol`, and when present `fixtureId`, `signalId`, and `priceEventId`.
Every time is displayed as stored UTC **and** KSA (`+03:00`). No credential,
token, account number, raw SQL parameter, or unbounded payload is logged.

The only event IDs are:

- `RUN_REQUESTED`, `RUN_QUALIFIED`, `RUN_REFUSED`, `RUN_FAILED`, `RUN_PROMOTED`;
- `FIXTURE_LOADED`;
- `PRICE_SPAN`;
- `DECISION`;
- `FIRST_DIVERGENCE`;
- `FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY`; and
- `POLICY_FINGERPRINT`.

Event payloads include the RPR-0.9.10 required fields: reason/evidence counts,
fixture hash/count, price-span boundaries/gap/refusal, decision path and
Section-3.3.1 step, input event IDs, expected/actual first divergence, attempted
class/method/table/URL, and policy source/configuration/profile identity.
Volume is bounded by E05. Tests assert one correctly correlated event per
occurrence.

A critical-safety mismatch is `ERROR` and **parity-not-earned**. Its mismatch
certificate and first divergence are published. It never becomes
`FAILED_REPLAY` merely because the result disagrees. `FAILED_REPLAY` means
technical error/cancellation/failed staging and discards staged artifacts.

## 7. Numbered validation matrix

| Test ID | Required assertion |
| --- | --- |
| T156-01 | 1m/5m/1h SELL and active-profit-lock SELL suppression match Production. |
| T156-02a | Signal-path TP, profit-lock, stop-loss and hard-floor precedence matches Section 3.3.1. |
| T156-02b | Price-path TP, stop-loss, dynamic lock, rebase floor, lock breach, normal exit and near-TP precedence matches Section 3.3.1. |
| T156-03 | Equal-time prices retain `(observed_at,id)`; E07 availability time governs same-boundary signal order. |
| T156-04 | Qualified price tail drains through `executionEnd`; incomplete tail refuses; after-end events are ignored. |
| T156-05 | Entry fixture execution time controls gating; no Replay request queries account tables. |
| T156-05a | Missing/malformed/wrong-date/fingerprint-mismatched policy profile is non-comparable/refused; no fallback to current settings. |
| T156-05b | Full timeline fixture covers add, stop/TP change, extension, harvest and profit-lock transition; first divergence ends that lifecycle metric. |
| T156-05c | Replay-local BUY mirror matches each recorded Production BUY allow/reject decision in the development fixture set, or publishes a mismatch without calling shared validation. |
| T156-06 | Lock-active positions do not evaluate/add when Production skips it. |
| T156-07a | Dynamic profit lock toggle on and off, values, extension and rebase match the profile fixtures. |
| T156-07b | Near-TP consumes dated exchange constraints and makes no HTTP/Binance call. |
| T156-08 | Missing historical context/data-quality/configuration evidence produces the right refusal/exclusion/bucket. |
| T156-08a | Missing or non-as-of sentiment provider configuration/health or prior indicator snapshot refuses analysis parity; no current value is substituted. |
| T156-09 | Direct and async account/HTTP/fallback access emits `FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY` and publishes nothing. |
| T156-09a | Static reachability/architecture test rejects any Replay-reachable repository, JDBC template, table-name access or HTTP client outside the approved allow-list. |
| T156-09b | Table allow-list test rejects every read/write/delete outside approved market-evidence and Replay-owned tables, except the exact run-owned and display-only `fix122_evaluation` operations in Section 4.2a. |
| T156-09c | Replay output/diagnostics expose only Replay fixture or position keys, never `paper_position_id` or another account identifier. |
| T156-09d | Entry consumption handles both cases: source-row signals use their saved IDs; `analyzeForRegression` signals use a stable Replay-owned key. A repeated entry is consumed without account evidence reads/writes. |
| T156-10 | Legacy/experimental observers cannot change a baseline result. |
| T156-11 | Charts/output query only Replay-owned data plus immutable overlay. |
| T156-12 | Ordinary and critical mismatches publish the correct first divergence; critical mismatches prevent the affected parity claim without becoming technical failure. |
| T156-13 | Replay-open/Production-open is censored as `OPEN_AT_REPLAY_END` with the final qualified mark price, price-event ID and time; Replay-open/Production-closed is a mismatch. No synthetic exit is created. |
| T156-14 | Staging/cancellation/resource refusal has correct terminal result and artifact handling. |
| T156-15 | Logging emits only the Section 6 event IDs, once per occurrence, with required UTC+KSA correlation fields. |
| T156-16 | Disposable-MySQL migration/schema validation is mandatory in the release stage and cannot silently skip. |

## 8. Completion criteria

Before any parity claim, actual changes must reconcile to the approved registry;
all required tests and E01-E10 evidence must exist; the qualified/excluded/
refused/non-comparable/censored buckets must be reported by cause and exit
class; and the frozen coverage/count/lower-bound requirements must pass
separately for both dimensions.

There must be **zero unexplained critical-safety mismatches** in stop-loss,
profit-lock, take-profit, near-TP ownership and duplicate-exit classes. A
qualified cohort may earn its applicable `ANALYSIS_PARITY` and/or
`LIFECYCLE_PARITY` dimension only. It never earns account or exact-fill parity.

## 9. Evidence status: E01 and E02 draft results

E01 and E02 are now consolidated into this preparation document. They are
read-only source evidence drafts, not approved evidence and not implementation
authority. Their register changes are incorporated in RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT and
remain subject to formal review.

### E01 - dependency audit: confirmed current violations

The current Replay path is not inside the approved boundary yet:

- `wallet_settings` is Replay-reachable through `ShadowProductionReplayService`,
  `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver`, and shared BUY validation.
- Near-TP reaches live Binance `/api/v3/exchangeInfo` through
  `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`.
- `wallet_trade` is read by the all-trades chart and proven-trade enrichment.
- If Replay scope propagation is absent, `EntryConsumptionPolicy` and
  `StopLossEvidenceStore` can fall through to Production-account reads.
- Replay currently reads, writes, and performs the scoped run-owned purge on
  shared `fix122_evaluation`; Section 4.2a defines the only allowed
  transitional exception and its delete policy. Replay can also write
  `shared_candle_input_audit` in shared-market LIVE mode.

The required future response is Replay-local policy/data adapters, immutable
policy and exchange-constraint fixtures, the allow-listed Replay data-access
layer, static reachability checks, and direct/async rejection tests. The E01
audit must also decide whether every shared-table write is replaced by a
Replay-owned record or made unreachable in the baseline.

RPR-0.9.12 records these formal boundary rows. They are not merely audit notes
because each one must be closed, tested and reconciled:

| Proposed row | E01 boundary violation | Required disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D47 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` reads `wallet_settings`. | Remove from baseline invocation or feed only the frozen Replay policy profile. |
| D48 | Replay reads, writes, and deletes from shared `fix122_evaluation`; the current delete is scoped to `test_run_id IS NOT NULL`, while Production also writes table rows. `StopLossEvidenceStore.productionDetails` separately reads `test_run_id IS NULL` rows for display. | Enforce Section 4.2a's exact run-owned and display-only allow-list/delete policy. The scoped purge and display query must not be reported as boundary violations; every other shared-table access remains forbidden. |
| D49 | Replay can write shared `shared_candle_input_audit` when shared-market mode is LIVE. | Make the path unreachable in baseline Replay; prove OFF/LIVE mode cannot bypass the boundary. |
| D50 | Missing Replay scope can fall through through `EntryConsumptionPolicy` to account evidence. | Fail closed through scope propagation and the Replay access allow-list. |
| D51 | Missing Replay scope can fall through through `StopLossEvidenceStore.latest` to `wallet_trade`. | Fail closed through scope propagation and the Replay access allow-list. |
| D52 | Missing scope can fall through to shared `execution_opportunity` reads/writes. | Route only to Replay-owned state or reject; never use shared mutable opportunity state. |
| D53 | Replay can read current sentiment-provider weights/health while evaluating historical time. | Require a dated immutable sentiment-provider configuration/health fixture; otherwise refuse analysis parity. |
| D54 | Replay can depend on a Production-saved prior indicator snapshot; a missing snapshot changes its result. | Include exact prior-snapshot identity/availability in the evidence qualifier; refuse when absent or not historically available. |
| D55 | Replay diagnostics display exposes `paper_position_id`, an account-table identifier. | Remove the account identifier from baseline Replay output and replace it with a Replay fixture/position key only. |
| D56 | Signals regenerated in memory by `analyzeForRegression` carry null IDs, making entry consumption inert and concealing the D50 fall-through path. Source-row signals loaded from saved `trade_signal` rows retain their saved IDs. | Give regenerated signals a stable Replay-owned signal key and explicit Replay-local entry-consumption state; preserve saved IDs for source-row signals and test both paths without querying or writing account evidence. |

### Consequence for approval gates

P02 and P03 remain pending. E01/E02 identify scope and register changes but do
not close E03-E10, do not prove runtime behaviour, and do not authorise a
Replay implementation. RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT must now be reviewed and frozen with
the required source/configuration/migration/worktree evidence before approval.

## 10. What was deliberately not done

- No Java source was modified.
- No SQL/Flyway migration was created or changed.
- No application or Replay configuration was changed.
- No database was queried, written, repaired, or migrated.
- No service was started/stopped and no deployment action occurred.
- No production behaviour was changed.

This preparation map authorises nothing. The canonical FIX-156 specification
remains **NOT APPROVED - IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN**.

## 11. Final pre-implementation static code review

### 11.1 Review identity and method

This section replaces the former standalone
`FIX-156_FINAL_PREIMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW_RPR-0.9.12.md` file. It is part of
this single consolidated document.

Reviewed specification: `C:\Users\jeane\Downloads\FIX-156-RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT.md`.
Its SHA-256 is the value recorded in this document header.

Review method: static source review only. No source, configuration, database,
or production data was changed. No compilation, test suite, database query,
Replay run, or deployment validation was performed.

### 11.2 Final verdict

RPR-0.9.12 is a coherent pre-implementation contract, but the current Replay
code does not yet meet it. The workspace contains legacy Replay behaviour
that FIX-156 is designed to replace. This review therefore cannot finalize or
deliver an implementation.

The permitted outcome is an implementation baseline and a precise blocker
list. Production behaviour remains unchanged. No FIX-156 implementation may
start until the specification-review, evidence, freeze, and authorization
gates in Sections 3, 7, 9, and 11.5 are complete.

### 11.3 Source areas reviewed

The final source check covered the primary Replay path and direct dependencies:

- `ShadowProductionReplayService`
- `RegressionTestService`
- `ExecutionReplayScope`
- `DynamicProfitLockService`
- `StopLossEvidenceStore`
- existing Replay-focused test files

This is not a claim that every repository class was exhaustively validated.
The authoritative full difference register remains RPR-0.9.12 rows D01-D56
and S01-S14.

### 11.4 Confirmed corrections and current non-compliance

The following specification corrections are supported by the source and must
remain in the implementation contract:

| Register item | Source result |
| --- | --- |
| D01 | Refuted as a Production/Replay difference: Production SELL validation also rejects non-1m signals through base validation. Retain as history only. |
| D08 | Refuted as a decision difference: Production wake-up behaviour is also 5m-only. Retain as history only. |
| D22 | Confirmed active difference: `DynamicProfitLockService.onTakeProfitExtended` passes literal `true` to `ProfitLockPolicy.evaluate`; `ShadowProductionReplayService.rebaseForExtendedTarget` passes `WalletSettings.isDynamicProfitLockEnabled()`. |
| D56 | Confirmed limited scope: only in-memory signals regenerated by `analyzeForRegression` can have a null id; source-row signals retain their saved `trade_signal` id. |

The following current-source behaviour is incompatible with the approved
FIX-156 target and remains open:

| Area | Current static evidence | Required final state |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Current wallet settings | `ShadowProductionReplayService` injects `WalletSettingsRepository`; `walletSettings()` loads row `1`. | Replay reads only frozen, extracted fixture settings. |
| Live exchange constraints | Replay calls `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.evaluate` for sell/harvest checks; it reaches Binance exchange-info data. | Fixture-backed historical exchange constraints; no live HTTP in Replay. |
| Shared diagnostics/evidence | `RegressionTestService` reads and executes `DELETE FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NOT NULL`; `StopLossEvidenceStore` inserts/reads that table and can read `wallet_trade`. | Enforce the exact run-owned `fix122_evaluation` exception/delete policy in Section 4.2a; all other shared access is forbidden and must be proven by allow-list and reachability tests. |
| Production trade enrichment/charting | `RegressionTestService.allTradesChart` and proven-trade detail paths query/enrich `wallet_trade`. | Historical extraction before a Replay run or a Replay-local replacement. |
| Profit-lock extension | Replay applies the current admin enable flag; Production forces enabled during extension rebase. | Frozen historical profile and exact Production input/precedence. |
| Opportunity lifecycle | Replay invokes `completePositionOpportunity` for price-driven exits as well as signal-driven exits. | Mirror the two Production decision orders and lifecycle transitions. |
| Signal identity/consumption | Scope is thread-local; regenerated signals may have null ids. | Replay-owned stable signal key and consumption state, while preserving saved source ids. |
| Parity measurement | Existing tests are limited and no demonstrated full decision-by-decision RPR-0.9.12 parity validator is invoked. | Registered comparator, divergence report, critical-safety gate, and fixture suite. |

Current discovered Replay-oriented tests include
`ShadowProductionReplayServiceFix120ParityTest`, `ReplayDatasetTest`, and
`ExecutionReplayScopeModeTest`. They do not demonstrate completion of
T156-01 through T156-16. In particular, the existing FIX-120 test mocks both
`WalletSettingsRepository` and `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, confirming
that those prohibited runtime dependencies are still part of the current
Replay design.

### 11.5 Mandatory gates before implementation

1. Complete P01 with exact source, configuration, migration, and clean-worktree hashes.
2. Review the exact RPR-0.9.12 revision and resolve all material findings.
3. Complete E03-E10 as read-only measured evidence, including historical settings, price-event coverage, signal availability, policy fingerprints, and resource limits.
4. Update the preparation registry only where that completed evidence requires it, while keeping RPR-0.9.12 as the canonical contract.
5. Obtain explicit user authorization for the exact reviewed and frozen revision.

The recorded P01 status reports a large dirty working tree. It cannot serve
as a reproducible baseline until the hashes and a clean baseline are
recorded.

### 11.6 Implementation boundary after authorization

FIX-156 may change Replay-owned code, fixtures, isolated Replay tables,
tests, and documentation only. It must not change Production trading logic,
Production wallet behaviour, Production database records, live Binance
behaviour, or deployment configuration.

The future implementation must include the registered structured logs with
UTC and KSA values, distinguish `ANALYSIS_PARITY` from `LIFECYCLE_PARITY`,
treat a business mismatch as parity-not-earned rather than `FAILED_REPLAY`,
and reject a parity claim for a critical-safety mismatch.

### 11.7 Consolidated conclusion

This document is now the single consolidated FIX-156 preparation and final
pre-implementation review artifact. It does not approve implementation.
Current code materially differs from the FIX-156 target by design. The next
safe action is completion of the evidence-and-freeze gates, followed by an
explicit implementation authorization for this exact RPR-0.9.12 revision or a
newer fully reviewed revision.

# Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign

**Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
**Status:** DRAFT Г?" NOT APPROVED Г?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
**Created:** 2026-10-10  
**Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review

## 1. Business objective

Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.

The exact intended flow is:

```text
historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
  -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
  -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
  -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
  -> replay-local position lifecycle
  -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
  -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
```

Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
`signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.

The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.

## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence

The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:

1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
   use several shared Production services.
2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
   positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
   labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
   does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
   event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
   position was open.
4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
   `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
   Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
   were not retained.
5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
   have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
   path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
   live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
   isolation.

## 3. Boundaries

### In scope

- Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
- Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
  daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
- Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
- Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
- Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
- Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.

### Out of scope

- Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
- Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
- Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
  external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
- FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
- Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.

## 4. Contractual modes

| Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
| `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
| `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |

The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.

## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility

A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
state are written unless all of the following hold:

1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
   USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
   limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
   and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
   global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
   that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
   symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
   current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
   position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
   `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
   consumed range.
6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
   enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
   enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
   derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
   mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
   requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
   unavailable and the mode is refused.

`SIMULATION` may use configured defaults or fixed starting capital but must
store each deviation in its output. It may never be displayed as parity.

### 5.1 Wallet isolation boundary

Replay never reads Production `wallet_asset`, `wallet_settings`,
`wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, daily-statistics,
or wallet-snapshot rows as a decision input. The existing data does not retain
complete historical wallet state and is deliberately irrelevant to a Replay
account. A user-selected/versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` is the only account
input. This preserves safe isolation while exercising the same pure sizing and
position-management formulas.

The profile also contains symbol execution constraints used by shared
paper-order guards. `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` must be split into a pure
minimum-notional evaluator plus a Production-only exchange-constraint provider;
Replay receives immutable profile values and may not issue Binance HTTP.

## 6. Agreed behaviour changes

### 6.1 Replay starting state

Replace hard-coded starting capital with an immutable `ReplayStartingState`.
It is seeded from the selected, versioned `ReplayAccountProfile` and stored
under the replay run ID before processing begins. Shadow wallet/position
services consume only this replay-local state and replay tables.

The run-start state must include profile ID/version, creation timestamp, version/config fingerprint,
virtual USDT/asset balances, daily-budget state, daily loss state, virtual
managed positions, virtual paper positions, replay idempotency keys, and a
versioned wallet/settings/configuration fingerprint. It may not be refreshed
from Production while a run is executing.

### 6.2 Shared paper-wallet business authority

Extract or complete pure shared policies for:

- initial and progressive sizing;
- reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, maximum-new-position, and global
  maximum-open-position controls;
- buy/sell/partial-harvest quantity and cost-basis calculations;
- position average entry, target/stop updates, profit-lock state, and near-TP
  state transitions;
- idempotency-key interpretation.

Production retains its current transactional repositories. Replay receives
replay-local adapters implementing the same policy inputs/outputs. No policy
may branch on `replay=true` to produce a different business decision.

### 6.3 Price evidence

Replace the boolean/label derived from Г?oany event existsГ?? with a coverage
result containing requested interval, covered interval, gaps, accepted event
count, rejected/unresolved count, and every position-open interval requiring
protection.

`BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` rejects incomplete coverage. `SIMULATION` may use
signal-price fallback only with the persisted reason `PRICE_PATH_INCOMPLETE`.

### 6.4 Historical decision evidence

Persist versioned derivatives and order-book observations required by
Production, or mark affected historical intervals ineligible for business-logic
parity. Replay must use the same normalized evaluator and only evidence at or
before the replay clock. It must never call live Binance endpoints.

### 6.5 Event ordering and time

Use canonical UTC `observed_at` and event IDs for price ordering. Define a
single deterministic tie order matching ProductionГ?Ts committed sequence:

1. accepted price protection event;
2. resulting position/wallet state;
3. closed-candle analysis signal;
4. signal-driven position action.

If durable evidence cannot establish the required tie order, business-logic parity
must refuse the run. Replay timestamps remain presentation-neutral UTC;
KSA conversion belongs only to UI/reporting.

### 6.6 Retired Replay-only behaviour

The following are **candidates for removal**, subject to complete-reference
review and approval. They must be removed from baseline parity runs now; any
future research requires an independently approved module and run mode.

| Registry ID | Candidate | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-RM-01 | `DefensiveRiskReductionReplayObserver` / FIX-11K | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-02 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` / FIX-11Q | Remove source, controller/UI/output tables only after archive/export compatibility is proved. |
| RPR-RM-03 | pre-FIX-122 replay revision selector | Remove the UI/API option and old comparison path; Production-current FIX-122 remains the only parity rule. |
| RPR-RM-04 | `EXPERIMENTAL` logic mode if no approved user-facing experiment consumes it | Remove after exact reference search; otherwise retain as an isolated framework with no baseline access. |

`ReplayDataSource.DATABASE` versus `DATASET` is not retired business logic:
both must remain only if a test proves equal outputs. It is a data-access
implementation choice, not an old Production-rule selector.

## 7. Implementation change registry

No implementation item is authorized until this exact revision is approved.

| ID | Area | Required change |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-01 | run contract | Add persisted mode, evidence status, account profile/state ID, version/config fingerprint, and refusal reason to the replay run. |
| RPR-02 | replay account schema/service | Add `ReplayAccountProfile` and immutable run-start-state tables. Seed virtual state only from the selected Replay profile. |
| RPR-03 | wallet authority | Extract/reuse pure paper-wallet policy and provide Production/replay persistence adapters; remove direct Replay reads of `WalletSettingsRepository`. |
| RPR-03a | execution constraints | Split minimum-notional evaluation from live exchange constraint acquisition; inject immutable profile constraints into Replay and prohibit Replay Binance HTTP. |
| RPR-04 | shadow replay | Replace fixed capital/local single-position assumptions with replay-local state and multi-symbol/global-limit coordinator. |
| RPR-05 | price evidence | Add interval coverage validator and replace the current any-event exact-price label. |
| RPR-06 | historical context | Add versioned persistence/lookup/qualification for order book and derivatives, or explicit parity refusal. |
| RPR-07 | timing/order | Persist/use deterministic committed event ordering and reject unknown ordering for parity runs. |
| RPR-08 | certificate | Add immutable parity certificate containing field comparisons, evidence counts, deviations, and final claim. |
| RPR-09 | removals | Execute only the approved RPR-RM entries, including migration/archive compatibility. |
| RPR-10 | UI/API | Make mode explicit; default to business-logic parity; display refusal/deviation/certificate details. |
| RPR-11 | tests | Add unit, integration, MySQL, complete scenario, no-look-ahead, and Production/Replay comparison tests below. |
| RPR-12 | docs | Update architecture, rules, changelog, investigation, operational runbook, and parity contract. |

## 8. Error handling, concurrency, and transactions

- Replay-state creation is isolated and must be consistent for one profile version.
- Business-logic-parity evidence validation happens before replay execution; no
  partial shadow run may be labelled a parity run.
- Replay output writes are transactionally isolated by `test_run_id`; a failed
  run records its failure and certificate status without changing Production.
- Replay must not lock Production wallet/position rows for business mutation.
- Replay-profile/state reads must not expose credentials and must log only IDs,
  UTC times, symbol counts, mode, and evidence result.
- Cancellation preserves isolated run evidence and cannot leave a completed
  parity certificate.

## 9. Logging and observability registry

| ID | Event | Mandatory fields |
|---|---|---|
| RPR-L-01 | virtual starting state created/refused | runId, mode, profile ID/version, virtual balances/position counts, config fingerprint, reason |
| RPR-L-02 | evidence qualified/refused | runId, symbol(s), from/to UTC, candle/price/orderbook/derivatives status, gap counts |
| RPR-L-03 | shadow execution decision | runId, symbol, signal/candle ID, decision code, shared-policy source, replay state ID |
| RPR-L-04 | parity mismatch | runId, field, Production value, replay value, evidence reference, severity |
| RPR-L-05 | certificate completed | runId, claim, matched/mismatched field counts, simulation deviations, duration |
| RPR-L-06 | retired path blocked | runId, removed/experimental feature ID, requested source, outcome |

## 10. Database/configuration/trading impact

- **Database:** additive replay account/snapshot/certificate/evidence schema and any
  required durable historical derivatives observations. Applied migrations are
  never edited. Archive migration/retention is mandatory before deletion of
  retired Replay-only output tables.
- **Configuration:** add replay mode/evidence policy only. Production defaults
  and trading configuration remain unchanged.
- **Trading behaviour:** NONE. Production is read-only to this feature and no
  live/paper Production decision, wallet mutation, position, or protection
  rule may change.
- **Replay behaviour:** baseline becomes evidence-qualified business-logic parity
  or explicitly labelled simulation; retired experimental paths are removed or
  isolated only as approved above.

## 11. Test and validation registry

| ID | Required proof |
|---|---|
| RPR-T-01 | The same profile inputs produce equal Production/replay sizing, reserve, daily budget, daily-loss, and global-position-limit decisions through shared policies. |
| RPR-T-01a | Replay refuses `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` when no immutable replay account profile is selected; it must never silently use a Production wallet row. |
| RPR-T-02 | A missing, unresolved, rejected, or coverage-gapped price event refuses business-logic parity and labels simulation correctly. |
| RPR-T-03 | Price-event/signal same-time ordering matches Production for TP, SL, profit lock, normal exit, and signal SELL. |
| RPR-T-04 | End-to-end initial BUY, progressive add, target extension, rebase, stop, profit lock, near-TP partial harvest, terminal exit, and P&L lifecycle match field-for-field. |
| RPR-T-05 | Historical order book/derivatives use only as-of persisted data; missing evidence refuses parity; no live HTTP occurs. |
| RPR-T-05a | Replay never reads a Production wallet settings row or calls a Binance minimum-notional endpoint; profile values drive the same pure minimum-order decision. |
| RPR-T-06 | Multi-symbol global wallet/max-position test proves no isolated-symbol bypass. |
| RPR-T-07 | Cancellation/error tests prove Production tables remain unchanged and certificates cannot falsely pass. |
| RPR-T-08 | MySQL migration and transaction tests use a disposable database. |
| RPR-T-09 | Dataset and database replay inputs produce equivalent output for the same qualified evidence, or one path is removed. |
| RPR-T-10 | Removal tests prove baseline Replay cannot invoke FIX-11K, FIX-11Q, or pre-FIX-122 paths. |
| RPR-T-11 | Real historical parity suite compares selected known Production windows and produces stored certificates. |

## 12. Risks, rollback, and acceptance

- Risk: historical data required for verified parity may not exist. Mitigation:
  refuse parity and use clearly labelled simulation; do not invent evidence.
- Risk: snapshot schema increases storage. Mitigation: bounded retention and
  archive strategy, never deletion of active/investigation evidence.
- Risk: retiring observers breaks historical UI/archive reads. Mitigation:
  migration and archive/export compatibility tests before removal.
- Rollback: disable new business-logic-parity mode and retain existing archived
  output; Production requires no rollback because it is never mutated.

Acceptance requires: all registry IDs reconciled; no unregistered code change;
all test evidence green; disposable MySQL validation; complete source review by
Claude; Codex verification of the exact reviewed revision; and explicit user
authorization after both agents approve this same document.

### src/main/java/com/crypto/service/TradeExecutionValidationService.java
40:     private static final String TREND_INTERVAL = "1h";
41:     private static final Duration ONE_MINUTE_TRANSITION_MAX_AGE = Duration.ofMinutes(10);
42:     private static final Duration FIVE_MINUTE_MAX_AGE = Duration.ofMinutes(20);
43:     private static final Duration ONE_HOUR_MAX_AGE = Duration.ofHours(3);
44: 
45:     // FIX-11G: this mirrors the existing global CHASE_ENTRY_CUTOFF in
46:     // ExecutionIntelligenceService. We deliberately reuse the proven execution-quality
47:     // boundary instead of inventing a new confidence/quality threshold for this one route.
48:     // The downstream global Entry Quality guard still remains authoritative and re-checks it.
49:     private static final int BALANCED_TRANSITIONAL_MIN_ENTRY_QUALITY = 50;
50: 
51:     private final TradeSignalRepository signalRepository;
52:     private final WalletSettingsRepository settingsRepository;
53:     private final EntryConsumptionPolicy entryConsumptionPolicy;
54:     private final HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService htfAssessment;
55:     @Autowired(required = false)
56:     private ExecutionReplayScope replayScope;

62:     public ValidationResult validateBuy(TradeSignal executionSignal, int entryQualityScore) {
63:         ValidationResult structural = validateBaseSignal(executionSignal, true);
64:         if (!structural.allowed()) return structural;
65: 
66:         WalletSettings settings = settings();
67:         if (settings.isRequireNewBuyTransition()) {
68:             TradeSignal previous = previousSignal(executionSignal.getSymbol(), EXECUTION_INTERVAL, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt()).orElse(null);
69:             // FIX-112A: a bullish label alone is not a consumed entry. Only keep
70:             // BUY_CONTINUATION protection when that exact previous signal has an
71:             // executed BUY (Production) or executed shadow BUY (Replay).
72:             if (isFresh(previous, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt(), ONE_MINUTE_TRANSITION_MAX_AGE)
73:                     && isBullish(previous.getDecision())
74:                     && entryConsumptionPolicy.resolve(previous.getId()) != EntryConsumptionState.NOT_CONSUMED) {
75:                 return ValidationResult.reject("BUY_CONTINUATION",
76:                         "BUY was not executed because a recent bullish opportunity for this symbol was already consumed. "
77:                                 + "A new WATCH/NEUTRAL/SELL -> BUY transition is required.");
78:             }
79:         }
80: 
81:         TradeSignal fiveMinute = latestAtOrBefore(executionSignal, CONFIRMATION_INTERVAL);
82:         if (!isFresh(fiveMinute, executionSignal.getGeneratedAt(), FIVE_MINUTE_MAX_AGE)) {
83:             return ValidationResult.reject("MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION",
84:                     "No fresh 5m confirmation was available for the 1m BUY signal.");
85:         }
380: 
381:     private boolean isFresh(TradeSignal signal, Instant reference, Duration maximumAge) {
382:         return signal != null
383:                 && signal.getGeneratedAt() != null
384:                 && !signal.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(reference)
385:                 && !signal.getGeneratedAt().isBefore(reference.minus(maximumAge));
386:     }
387: 
388:     private boolean isBullish(SignalDecision decision) {
389:         return decision == SignalDecision.BUY || decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_BUY;
390:     }
391: 
392:     private boolean isBearish(SignalDecision decision) {
393:         return decision == SignalDecision.SELL || decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_SELL;
394:     }
395: 
396:     private boolean isWatchOrNeutral(SignalDecision decision) {
397:         return decision == SignalDecision.WATCH || decision == SignalDecision.NEUTRAL;
398:     }
399: 
400:     private WalletSettings settings() {
401:         return settingsRepository.findById(1L)
402:                 .orElseGet(() -> WalletSettings.builder()
403:                         .id(1L)
404:                         .executionProfile(ExecutionProfile.BALANCED.name())
405:                         .requireNewBuyTransition(true)
406:                         .dynamicProfitLockEnabled(true)
407:                         .profitLockActivationPercent(BigDecimal.valueOf(70))
408:                         .profitLockInitialPercent(BigDecimal.valueOf(40))
409:                         .profitLockTrailStepPercent(BigDecimal.valueOf(10))
410:                         .build());
411:     }
412: 
413:     private ValidationResult cap(ValidationResult result, HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment) {
414:         if (!result.allowed() || assessment.maxInitialPercent()==null) return result;
415:         int capped=Math.min(result.positionPercent(),assessment.maxInitialPercent());
416:         if (capped != result.positionPercent() && fix154Metrics != null) fix154Metrics.softCappedEntry();
417:         return capped==result.positionPercent()?result:ValidationResult.allow(capped,result.code(),result.explanation()+" FIX-154 soft-HTF cap="+capped+"%.");
418:     }

### src/main/java/com/crypto/wallet/service/BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.java
1: package com.crypto.wallet.service;
2: 
3: import com.crypto.client.binance.BinanceMarketDataClient;
4: import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
5: import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
6: import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
7: 
8: import java.math.BigDecimal;
9: import java.math.MathContext;
10: import java.util.Locale;
11: 
12: /**
13:  * FIX-11T minimum-executable-order guard shared by Production Paper and Replay.
14:  *
15:  * The project intentionally models Paper/Replay as real Binance trading with only the
16:  * final connector missing.  Therefore a Near-TP partial harvest that Binance would reject
17:  * for being below the symbol's minimum notional is not simulated.  No alternative exit
18:  * size is invented: the position simply continues through its existing management logic.
19:  *
20:  * This class deliberately checks only the agreed minimum executable notional boundary.
21:  * It does not add a new Near-TP-specific step-size/precision model.
22:  */
23: @Component
24: @RequiredArgsConstructor
25: @Slf4j
26: public class BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy {
27:     private static final MathContext MC = MathContext.DECIMAL64;
28: 
29:     private final BinanceMarketDataClient binanceMarketDataClient;
30:     public Evaluation evaluate(String symbol, BigDecimal quantity, BigDecimal price) {
31:         if (symbol == null || symbol.isBlank() || quantity == null || quantity.signum() <= 0
32:                 || price == null || price.signum() <= 0) {
33:             return new Evaluation(false, "INVALID_ORDER_INPUT", BigDecimal.ZERO, null);
34:         }
35:         String pair = symbol.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
36:         BigDecimal notional = quantity.multiply(price, MC);
37:         BigDecimal minimum;
38:         try {
39:             minimum = binanceMarketDataClient.getMinimumExecutableNotional(pair);
40:         } catch (RuntimeException ex) {
41:             // Fail safe. Missing exchange constraints must never be converted into permission
42:             // to create an order that real Binance might reject.
43:             log.warn("FIX-11T Binance minimum executable amount unavailable: symbol={}, requestedNotional={}, error={}",
44:                     pair, notional, ex.getMessage());
45:             return new Evaluation(false, "BINANCE_MINIMUM_UNAVAILABLE", notional, null);
46:         }
47:         if (notional.compareTo(minimum) < 0) {
48:             return new Evaluation(false, "BELOW_BINANCE_MINIMUM", notional, minimum);
49:         }
50:         return new Evaluation(true, "EXECUTABLE", notional, minimum);
51:     }
52: 
53:     public record Evaluation(boolean executable, String code, BigDecimal requestedNotional, BigDecimal minimumNotional) {
54:     }
55: }

459:     @Transactional
460:     public ExecutionDecision evaluateBuy(TradeSignal signal, int currentAllocationPercent, String currentStage) {
461:         if (stopLossEvidence == null) return evaluateBuyInternal(signal, currentAllocationPercent, currentStage);
462:         return stopLossEvidence.evaluate(signal, currentAllocationPercent,
463:                 () -> evaluateBuyInternal(signal, currentAllocationPercent, currentStage));
464:     }
465: 
466:     private ExecutionDecision evaluateBuyInternal(TradeSignal signal, int currentAllocationPercent, String currentStage) {
467:         if (signal == null || signal.getGeneratedAt() == null) {
468:             return ExecutionDecision.reject("INVALID_SIGNAL", "Signal is missing required execution data.");
469:         }
470:         if (!EXECUTION_INTERVAL.equals(signal.getInterval())) {
471:             return ExecutionDecision.observe("CONTEXT_ONLY", "Only fresh 1m signals may trigger a BUY execution.");
472:         }
473: 
474:         if (isBearish(signal.getDecision())) {
475:             Evidence evidence = evidence(signal);
476:             if (isHardBearishReversal(signal, evidence)) {
477:                 saveOpportunity(signal, evidence, "CANCELLED", "OPPORTUNITY_MEMORY", 0,
478:                         "BEARISH_REVERSAL",
479:                         "BUY opportunity cancelled because bearish evidence is strong or confirmed by 5m/1h context.");
480:                 return ExecutionDecision.reject("BEARISH_REVERSAL",
481:                         "Current bearish evidence is strong enough to invalidate the BUY opportunity.", evidence);
482:             }
483: 
484:             if (evidence.opportunityHealth() < OPPORTUNITY_HEALTH_MIN_TO_KEEP) {
485:                 saveOpportunity(signal, evidence, "CANCELLED", "OPPORTUNITY_MEMORY", 0,
486:                         "OPPORTUNITY_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED",
487:                         "BUY opportunity health fell below the minimum after bearish/aging penalties.");
488:                 return ExecutionDecision.reject("OPPORTUNITY_HEALTH_EXHAUSTED",
489:                         "Opportunity memory decayed below the safe minimum and was cancelled.", evidence);
490:             }
491: 
492:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence, "WEAKENING", "OPPORTUNITY_MEMORY", 0,
493:                     "SOFT_BEARISH_INTERRUPTION",
494:                     "A brief 1m bearish interruption reduced opportunity evidence instead of erasing it. "
495:                             + "Health=" + evidence.opportunityHealth() + "/100, evidence=" + evidence.evidenceScore()
496:                             + ". A new supportive signal is required before execution.");
497:             return ExecutionDecision.weakening("SOFT_BEARISH_INTERRUPTION",
498:                     "Bearish 1m evidence weakened the opportunity but did not invalidate supportive 5m/1h context.", evidence);
499:         }
500: 
501:         // Non-ATR safety vetoes remain absolute except for one deliberately tiny,
502:         // fully-auditable BTC-only exception. An exceptional independent-strength
503:         // reversal may take a 10-15% probe when BTC is bearish, but only if the
504:         // asset's own 5m/1h context is no longer bearish and every other hard gate
505:         // plus ATR immediate-entry permission still passes.
506:         HardRiskBlock nonAtrHardBlock = nonAtrHardRiskBlock(signal);
507:         if (nonAtrHardBlock.blocked()) {
508:             Evidence evidence = evidence(signal);
509:             ExecutionDecision exceptionalProbe = exceptionalStrengthProbeDecision(signal, evidence, nonAtrHardBlock);
510:             if (exceptionalProbe != null) {
511:                 EntryQuality probeQuality = assessEntryQuality(signal);
512:                 exceptionalProbe = applyExceptionalProbeEntryQualityGuard(exceptionalProbe, probeQuality);
513:                 saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
514:                         exceptionalProbe.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : exceptionalProbe.state(),
515:                         exceptionalProbe.source(), exceptionalProbe.positionPercent(),
516:                         exceptionalProbe.code(), exceptionalProbe.explanation());
517:                 return exceptionalProbe;
518:             }
519:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence, "BLOCKED", "HARD_RISK", 0,
520:                     nonAtrHardBlock.code(), nonAtrHardBlock.explanation());
521:             return ExecutionDecision.reject(nonAtrHardBlock.code(), nonAtrHardBlock.explanation(), evidence);
522:         }
523: 
524:         if (signal.getLatestPrice() == null || signal.getStopLoss() == null || signal.getTakeProfit() == null
525:                 || signal.getStopLoss().signum() <= 0 || signal.getTakeProfit().signum() <= 0) {
526:             Evidence evidence = evidence(signal);
527:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence, "BLOCKED", "HARD_RISK", 0,
528:                     "MISSING_RISK_PLAN", "Execution requires a fresh entry price, stop loss, and take-profit plan.");
529:             return ExecutionDecision.reject("MISSING_RISK_PLAN",
530:                     "Execution requires a fresh entry price, stop loss, and take-profit plan.", evidence);
531:         }
532: 
533:         // Evaluate deferred continuation BEFORE the ordinary ATR immediate-entry veto.
534:         // This route exists specifically for a previous analytically valid BUY that was
535:         // deferred by ATR waiting for a pullback. It still requires a current risk plan,
536:         // supportive 1m/5m/1h context, healthy opportunity state and >= 1:1 current R/R.
537:         Evidence evidence = evidence(signal);
538:         EntryQuality entryQuality = assessEntryQuality(signal);
539: 
540:         // Regression safety: a fresh signal that already qualifies for the normal direct-BUY
541:         // path must not be intercepted by the zero-allocation SCOUT_ENTRY branch. The scout is
542:         // exploratory authority for supportive signals that are not yet ordinary executable BUYs.
543:         // Progressive ADD stages remain unchanged because an existing allocation (> 0) still
544:         // reaches progressivePositionDecision exactly as before.
545:         //
546:         // This preserves the documented execution priority:
547:         // normal executable BUY -> IMMEDIATE_VALIDATION; otherwise consider an initial scout.
548:         boolean normalDirectBuyCandidate = currentAllocationPercent <= 0 && isDirectBuyCandidate(signal);
549:         ExecutionDecision progressive = normalDirectBuyCandidate
550:                 ? null
551:                 : progressivePositionDecision(
552:                         signal, evidence, entryQuality, currentAllocationPercent, currentStage);
553:         if (progressive != null) {
554:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
555:                     progressive.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : progressive.state(),
556:                     progressive.source(), progressive.positionPercent(),
557:                     progressive.code(), progressive.explanation());
558:             return progressive;
559:         }
560: 
561:         // When a wallet position is already open, only an explicit progressive add
562:         // may increase exposure. Normal initial-entry routes are not re-run.
563:         if (currentAllocationPercent > 0) {
564:             return ExecutionDecision.observe(
565:                     "POSITION_BUILDING_HOLD",
566:                     "An open position already exists at " + currentAllocationPercent
567:                             + "% allocation. No additional stage qualified on this signal.",
568:                     evidence);
569:         }
570: 
571:         // Breakout retracement handoff runs before the generic reversal retracement route.
572:         // It remembers a recent BREAKOUT/WAIT_FOR_RETRACEMENT setup, so the pullback
573:         // candle does not have to recreate the breakout's volume spike. Production and
574:         // replay both reach this exact method through the shared evaluateBuy path.
575:         ExecutionDecision breakoutRetracement = breakoutRetracementDecision(signal, evidence);
576:         if (breakoutRetracement != null) {
577:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
578:                     breakoutRetracement.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : breakoutRetracement.state(),
579:                     breakoutRetracement.source(), breakoutRetracement.positionPercent(),
580:                     breakoutRetracement.code(), breakoutRetracement.explanation());
581:             return breakoutRetracement;
582:         }
583: 
584:         // ATR retracement handoff: preserve a recent, high-quality BUY that ATR refused
585:         // to chase and execute only when price actually returns to ATR's own requested
586:         // retracement zone. The current 1m candle may be WATCH/NEUTRAL during the pullback;
587:         // the original thesis is revalidated with as-of 5m context instead of requiring
588:         // another BUY after price has already started moving again.
589:         ExecutionDecision retracement = reversalRetracementDecision(signal, evidence);
590:         if (retracement != null) {
591:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
592:                     retracement.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : retracement.state(),
593:                     retracement.source(), retracement.positionPercent(),
594:                     retracement.code(), retracement.explanation());
595:             return retracement;
596:         }
597: 
598:         ExecutionDecision deferred = deferredContinuationDecision(signal, evidence);
599:         if (deferred != null) {
600:             deferred = applyInitialEntryQualityGuard(deferred, entryQuality);
601:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
602:                     deferred.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : deferred.state(),
603:                     deferred.source(), deferred.positionPercent(),
604:                     deferred.code(), deferred.explanation());
605:             return deferred;
606:         }
607: 
608:         // A fresh 1h BUY plus supportive 5m/1m transition can justify a strictly reduced
609:         // entry even when ATR is still extended. This is intentionally evaluated before
610:         // the ordinary 5m-BUY ATR fallback because the 5m may still be WATCH during the
611:         // first minutes of a genuine higher-timeframe reversal.
612:         ExecutionDecision transition = higherTimeframeTransitionDecision(signal, evidence);
613:         if (transition != null) {
614:             TradeSignal five = latestAtOrBefore(signal, CONFIRMATION_INTERVAL, FIVE_MINUTE_MAX_AGE);
615:             EntryQuality transitionQuality = assessEntryQuality(signal, five == null ? signal.getAtrAtSignal() : five.getAtrAtSignal());
616:             transition = applyTransitionEntryQualityGuard(transition, transitionQuality);
617:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
618:                     transition.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : transition.state(),
619:                     transition.source(), transition.positionPercent(),
620:                     transition.code(), transition.explanation());
621:             return transition;
622:         }
623: 
624:         // A valid 5m BUY owns setup-level ATR risk. The 1m ATR still controls timing,
625:         // but it must not permanently kill a fresh 5m setup whose own ATR explicitly
626:         // permits immediate/reduced entry. If a fresh 1h context exists and is bearish,
627:         // it still vetoes this route. Missing 1h context does not manufacture a veto.
628:         TradeSignal setupAtrSignal = setupTimeframeAtrAuthoritySignal(signal, evidence);
629:         if (setupAtrSignal != null) {
630:             ExecutionDecision setupAtrAuthority = setupTimeframeAtrAuthorityDecision(signal, evidence, setupAtrSignal);
631:             // Entry-quality ATR extension must use the same setup timeframe that owns
632:             // ATR risk. Using the much smaller 1m ATR here can falsely label a valid
633:             // 5m setup as a 9-10 ATR chase even when the 5m risk plan permits entry.
634:             EntryQuality setupEntryQuality = assessEntryQuality(signal, setupAtrSignal.getAtrAtSignal());
635:             setupAtrAuthority = applyInitialEntryQualityGuard(setupAtrAuthority, setupEntryQuality);
636:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
637:                     setupAtrAuthority.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : setupAtrAuthority.state(),
638:                     setupAtrAuthority.source(), setupAtrAuthority.positionPercent(),
639:                     setupAtrAuthority.code(), setupAtrAuthority.explanation());
640:             return setupAtrAuthority;
641:         }
642: 
643:         // For every remaining non-continuation path, ATR immediate-entry permission remains mandatory.
644:         if (!signal.isAtrImmediateEntryAllowed()) {
645:             saveOpportunity(signal, evidence, "BLOCKED", "HARD_RISK", 0,
646:                     "ATR_ENTRY_BLOCKED", "ATR risk controls do not allow immediate entry.");
647:             return ExecutionDecision.reject("ATR_ENTRY_BLOCKED",
648:                     "ATR risk controls do not allow immediate entry.", evidence);
649:         }
650: 
651:         // Fast path: a normal fresh BUY can execute immediately using the configured profile.
652:         if (isDirectBuyCandidate(signal)) {
653:             TradeExecutionValidationService.ValidationResult validation = validationService.validateBuy(signal, entryQuality.score());
654:             if (validation.allowed()) {
655:                 ExecutionDecision postBearishGuard = balancedEarlyPostBearishGuard(signal, evidence, entryQuality, validation);
656:                 if (postBearishGuard != null) {
657:                     saveOpportunity(signal, evidence,
658:                             postBearishGuard.allowed() ? "CONFIRMED" : postBearishGuard.state(),
659:                             postBearishGuard.source(), postBearishGuard.positionPercent(),
660:                             postBearishGuard.code(), postBearishGuard.explanation());
661:                     return postBearishGuard;
662:                 }
663: 
664:                 // FIX-041 / ACEUSDT 2026-08-22 13:28 KSA:
665:                 // BALANCED_EARLY is an early-entry profile by definition. If the independent
666:                 // Entry Quality model already classifies the price as LATE_ENTRY, executing
667:                 // this route would contradict its purpose and can turn a well-detected trend
668:                 // into a chase near exhaustion. Keep every other proven initial-entry route
669:                 // unchanged: setup-timeframe ATR authority, confirmation wake-up, accumulated
670:                 // evidence, recovery probes and normal non-BALANCED_EARLY BUYs still use the
671:                 // existing quality guard. Production and Replay share this exact method.
672:                 if ("BALANCED_EARLY".equals(validation.code())
673:                         && "LATE_ENTRY".equals(entryQuality.classification())) {
674:                     ExecutionDecision lateBalancedEarly = ExecutionDecision.building(
675:                             "BALANCED_EARLY_LATE_ENTRY_BLOCKED",
2580:         opportunity.setExecutionSource(source);
2581:         opportunity.setRecommendedPositionPercent(positionPercent);
2582:         opportunity.setDecisionCode(code);
2583:         opportunity.setDecisionExplanation(explanation);
2584:         opportunity.setUpdatedAt(now);

### src/main/java/com/crypto/execution/service/StopLossEvidenceStore.java
1: package com.crypto.execution.service;
2: 
3: import org.springframework.jdbc.core.JdbcTemplate;
4: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
5: import org.springframework.transaction.PlatformTransactionManager;
6: import org.springframework.transaction.TransactionDefinition;
7: import org.springframework.transaction.support.TransactionTemplate;
8: import javax.sql.DataSource;
9: import java.sql.Timestamp;
10: import java.time.Instant;
11: import java.util.List;
12: import java.util.Map;
13: 
14: /** FIX-122: bounded, non-locking committed ledger reads; no managed-position row locks.
15:  * STOP_LOSS is the terminal reason written by executeSignalLinkedExit. The distinct
16:  * POSITION_STOP_LOSS and partial-harvest reasons are deliberately not included.
17:  */
18: @Service
19: public class StopLossEvidenceStore {
20:     private final JdbcTemplate jdbc;
21:     private final TransactionTemplate read;
22:     private final TransactionTemplate write;
23: 
24:     public StopLossEvidenceStore(DataSource dataSource, PlatformTransactionManager manager) {
25:         jdbc = new JdbcTemplate(dataSource);
26:         jdbc.setQueryTimeout(2);
27:         read = new TransactionTemplate(manager);
28:         read.setPropagationBehavior(TransactionDefinition.PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW);
29:         read.setIsolationLevel(TransactionDefinition.ISOLATION_READ_COMMITTED);
30:         read.setReadOnly(true);
31:         read.setTimeout(2);
32:         write = new TransactionTemplate(manager);
33:         write.setPropagationBehavior(TransactionDefinition.PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW);
34:         write.setTimeout(2);
35:     }
36: 
37:     public StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary latest(String symbol, Instant asOf) {
38:         return read.execute(status -> jdbc.query("""
39:                 SELECT id, executed_at FROM wallet_trade
40:                 WHERE symbol=? AND status='EXECUTED' AND side='SELL'
41:                   AND execution_reason='STOP_LOSS' AND executed_at<=?
42:                 ORDER BY executed_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1
43:                 """, (rs, row) -> new StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary(rs.getLong(1),
44:                 rs.getTimestamp(2).toInstant()), symbol, Timestamp.from(asOf))
45:                 .stream().findFirst().orElse(null));
46:     }
47: 
48:     public void append(Long runId, String symbol, Long signalId, Instant at, String stage, String payload) {
49:         // Separate audit transaction survives a caller rollback; it records an evaluation,
50:         // never asserts that a BUY/SELL actually executed. No FK to wallet rows is acquired.
51:         write.executeWithoutResult(status -> jdbc.update("""
52:                 INSERT INTO fix122_evaluation(test_run_id,symbol,signal_id,evaluated_at,stage,payload)
53:                 VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?)
54:                 """, runId, symbol, signalId, Timestamp.from(at), stage, payload));
55:     }
56: 
57:     public List<Map<String,Object>> replayDetails(long runId) {
58:         return jdbc.queryForList("SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id=? ORDER BY id", runId);
59:     }
60: 
61:     public List<Map<String,Object>> productionDetails(String symbol, Instant from, Instant to) {
62:         return jdbc.queryForList("""
63:                 SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NULL AND symbol=?
64:                 AND evaluated_at>=? AND evaluated_at<=? ORDER BY id
65:                 """, symbol, Timestamp.from(from), Timestamp.from(to));
66:     }
67: }

16: /**
17:  * Thread-bound data/persistence override used only by Administration historical replay.
18:  *
19:  * The execution algorithms remain the production services. While this scope is active,
20:  * their signal lookups are resolved exclusively from replay-generated signals as-of the
21:  * current replay timestamp, and opportunity persistence is redirected to the supplied
22:  * shadow sink. Production repositories are never read/written by the scoped execution path.
23:  */
24: @Component
25: public class ExecutionReplayScope {
26: 
27:     /** FIX-109: Production parity is the default replay contract. Experimental mode is
28:      * explicit so replay-only candidate rules can never contaminate a parity result. */
29:     public enum ReplayLogicMode { PRODUCTION_PARITY, EXPERIMENTAL }
30: 
31:     private final ThreadLocal<State> state = new ThreadLocal<>();
32: 
33:     public Scope open(long runId, List<TradeSignal> replaySignals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink) {
34:         return open(runId, replaySignals, opportunitySink, ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY);
35:     }
36: 
37:     public Scope open(long runId, List<TradeSignal> replaySignals, Consumer<ExecutionOpportunity> opportunitySink,
38:                       ReplayLogicMode logicMode) {
39:         if (state.get() != null) throw new IllegalStateException("Execution replay scope is already active on this thread");
40:         List<TradeSignal> immutable = replaySignals == null ? List.of() : replaySignals.stream()
41:                 .filter(s -> s != null && s.getGeneratedAt() != null)
42:                 .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt))
43:                 .toList();
44:         state.set(new State(runId, immutable, opportunitySink, logicMode == null ? ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY : logicMode));
45:         return new Scope();
46:     }
47: 
48:     /** FIX-122: run-local executed boundaries, independent of Production ledger/history. */
49:     public void recordStop(String symbol, long positionId, Instant at, String reason) {
50:         if (!"STOP_LOSS".equals(reason)) return;
51:         State s=required();
52:         StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary previous=s.stops.get(symbol);
53:         if (previous==null || !at.isBefore(previous.executedAt()))
54:             s.stops.put(symbol,new StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary(positionId,at));
55:     }
56:     public StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary stopBoundary(String symbol, Instant asOf) {
57:         StopLossEvidencePolicy.Boundary b=required().stops.get(symbol);
58:         return b!=null && !b.executedAt().isAfter(asOf) ? b : null;
59:     }
60:     public Instant referenceInstant() { return required().reference; }
61:     public boolean fix122Enabled() { return required().fix122Enabled; }
62:     public void fix122Enabled(boolean value) { required().fix122Enabled=value; }
63: 
64:     public boolean active() { return state.get() != null; }
65:     public long runId() { return required().runId; }
66:     public ReplayLogicMode logicMode() { return required().logicMode; }
67:     public boolean experimental() { return active() && required().logicMode == ReplayLogicMode.EXPERIMENTAL; }
68:     public boolean productionParity() { return active() && required().logicMode == ReplayLogicMode.PRODUCTION_PARITY; }
69: 
70:     public void reference(Instant reference) { required().reference = reference; }
71: 
72:     /** FIX-056: expose the exact latest replay market-price event to the shared execution-price authority. */
73:     public void referencePrice(String symbol, Instant observedAt, java.math.BigDecimal price) {
74:         State s = required();
75:         s.reference = observedAt;
76:         s.latestPrice = new ReplayPrice(symbol, observedAt, price);
77:     }
78: 
79:     public Optional<ReplayPrice> latestPrice() {
80:         State s = required();

82:     }
83: 
84:     /** FIX-112A: Replay-local execution ledger keyed to the exact triggering signal. */

86:         return active() && signalId != null && required().consumedSignalIds.contains(signalId);
87:     }
88:

90:     public void markEntryConsumed(Long signalId) {
91:         if (signalId != null) required().consumedSignalIds.add(signalId);
92:     }
93: 
94:     /**
95:      * Adds a freshly generated replay signal to the as-of context stream. Signal generation
96:      * uses this incrementally so the production confluence/BTC services can resolve only
97:      * context that had genuinely been generated by the current replay timestamp.
98:      */
99:     public void appendSignal(TradeSignal signal) {
100:         if (signal == null || signal.getGeneratedAt() == null) return;
101:         State s = required();
102:         s.signals.add(signal);
103:         s.signals.sort(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt));
104:     }
105: 
106:     public List<TradeSignal> recent(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference, int limit) {
107:         State s = required();
108:         Instant ref = reference != null ? reference : s.reference;
109:         if (ref == null) return List.of();
110:         return s.signals.stream()
111:                 .filter(x -> symbol.equals(x.getSymbol()) && interval.equals(x.getInterval()))
112:                 .filter(x -> !x.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(ref))
113:                 .sorted(Comparator.comparing(TradeSignal::getGeneratedAt).reversed())
114:                 .limit(limit)
115:                 .toList();
